cat > README.md <<'EOF'
# @merchant/theme-engine

Section registry and React renderers for the AI-built storefront (SPEC §12).
Owner: WS-F.

## Safe by construction

A theme is **data, never code**. Sections receive validated `settings` and the
data the storefront injected — nothing else: no arbitrary HTML, no injected CSS,
no runtime fetches. Every string of HTML goes through `<RichHtml>`, which
sanitizes with an allowlist (`shared/sanitize.ts`). This is what makes it safe
to let a model author a storefront.

## The pipeline

### 👤 User

`minimal prompt → single careful diff pass → ≤15 findings`

You are reviewing a pull request for real bugs. Run `git diff @{upstream}...HEAD` (or `git diff main...HEAD` / `git diff HEAD~1`
if there's no upstream) to get the unified diff under review. If there are
uncommitted changes, or the range diff is empty, also run `git diff HEAD` and
include the working-tree changes in scope — the review often runs before the
commit. If a PR number, branch name, or file path was passed as an argument,
review that target instead. Treat this diff as the review scope.

Review the diff as a careful senior engineer would: read every hunk, open the surrounding files for context as needed (Read, Grep, git log/blame/show), and hunt for correctness issues — wrong or inverted conditions, off-by-one, null/undefined dereference, missing `await`, dropped error handling, removed guards or validations, broken callers of changed functions, races. Prefer real failure modes over style; every finding needs a concrete scenario in which the code misbehaves.

When you are done, submit at most 15 findings via the ReportFindings tool, filling its fields as defined — for each: the file path and start line, a severity, and a comment that states the issue and the concrete scenario in which the code misbehaves. Quality over quantity: include everything you genuinely believe is a real issue, and nothing you don't.

After the tool call, also restate the findings in your final reply — one line each, `file:line — summary` — so they stay visible in sessions that do not render tool output.

### 🤖 Claude

**F1 — Theme engine core is merged** ([PR #7](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/7), squashed into `main` as `705f49e`). CI green, branch deleted.

## What shipped

**Render pipeline** — [render.tsx](packages/theme-engine/src/render.tsx): `renderPage(doc, page, data)`, `renderFooter`, `themeCssVariables`, and `googleFontsHref` (E2 needs it to load the two typefaces a theme names).

**The WS-E contract** — [context.ts](packages/theme-engine/src/context.ts): `SectionDataContext` (resolved product / collection / cart / handle lookups) plus typed **slots**. Sections are Server Components, so every interactive control — buy box, qty stepper, sort, dismiss — is a slot E2 owns. A missing slot renders nothing rather than a control that looks live and isn't.

**Shared primitives** — `ThemeButton`, `ProductCard`, `SectionShell`, `Price`, `RichHtml`, `productGridClass`, route helpers. F2 builds on these.

**Core sections, real implementations** — product-detail (three CSS-only gallery layouts, vendor/sku/share toggles, related row), collection-page (filters, sort, responsive grid, cursor pagination links), cart-page, footer, announcement-bar.

**Sanitizer** — `sanitize-html` behind `<RichHtml>`, the single audited injection point. Closes the promise the README had been making.

**Three presets** — aurora (seed default), monochrome, bloom. Authored as zod *input* and parsed per call, so an invalid preset is a CI failure rather than a broken storefront, and each caller gets an independent object to mutate.

## Tests (65, `--passWithNoTests` removed)

Written first, red bar confirmed before implementing. Presets parse `themeDocSchema` with zero `validateThemeDoc` problems; sanitizer strips `<script>`/`onerror`/`javascript:` and keeps formatting; `renderPage` smoke-renders every preset and every registered section type at schema defaults, with and without data. Plus a **token-discipline gate** that fails the build on a hardcoded colour anywhere in `sections/` or `shared/` — the workstream's #1 landmine, and it guards F2's 13 files too.

I mutation-tested the suite (hardcoded a colour, broke a preset's required section) and confirmed both fail it.

## Two decisions worth flagging

- **Button style is resolved at token time.** CSS cannot branch on the *value* of a custom property, so `themeCssVariables` derives `--theme-button-bg/-fg/-border` from `tokens.buttonStyle`.
- **`renderSection` now takes a data context.** The registry *map* is untouched — that map is what the no-edit rule protects. Logged in `DECISIONS.md`.

## Verification

Not just tests: I rendered all three presets against real fixtures using the actual Tailwind build and looked at them at desktop and mobile. Aurora, monochrome and bloom are visibly distinct (colour, radius, typeface, button treatment) — which is what H2 flow (d) needs. Found and fixed a mobile gap in the `grid` gallery layout that way. Self-review caught two React key collisions (duplicate footer headings, repeated image URLs).

## Two things for other agents

Both logged in `docs/AGENT-LOG.md`:

- **E2 must add `@source "../../../../packages/theme-engine/src";` to `apps/storefront/src/app/globals.css`.** Tailwind 4 doesn't scan `node_modules`, so without it none of my classes are emitted and every page renders unstyled. I left it because `globals.css` is E2's file.
- **H1**: presets reference exactly one collection handle, `featured`, enforced by a test. Aurora is `DEFAULT_PRESET`.

One note: C1 and D1 landed while I worked, so I rebased. During that I hit 3 failing `@merchant/pay` tests — that was my worktree missing a local `.env`, not a code problem. `cp .env.example .env` and all 60 pass. Final state: 176 tests green across the repo.

/**
 * A horizontal scroll-snap track: swipeable on touch, scrollable on desktop,

 * The native scrollbar is hidden because the dots (or the images themselves)
 * are the affordance — a scrollbar under a full-bleed image reads as a bug.
 * Note there is deliberately no `scroll-smooth` here: CSS smooth scrolling also
 * hijacks direct `scrollLeft` assignment, so the smoothness is decided per call
 * by the client island, which can honour `prefers-reduced-motion`.
 *

### 🤖 Claude

**F2 — Marketing sections is merged** ([PR #15](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/15), squashed into `main` as `2649ba9`). CI green, branch deleted.

## What shipped

All 13 marketing sections, implemented to the existing schemas without extending them: `hero` (background/left/right, four heights, overlay opacity, two buttons), `image-with-text`, `image-banner`, `slideshow`, `featured-collection`, `product-grid`, `collection-list`, `rich-text`, `testimonials`, `logo-list`, `newsletter`, `faq`, `contact`.

Four new shared primitives came out of building them, which F1's core sections now share too:
- **`ThemeImage`** — an image plus a layout-stable placeholder. Model-authored themes leave images null constantly, and a section that collapses looks broken.
- **`ProductSkeletonGrid` / `CardSkeletonGrid`** — what a grid renders when its handles resolve to nothing.
- **`SNAP_TRACK`** — the scroll-snap carousel track.
- **`ThemeButton variant="on-image"`** — readable over an image overlay.

**Exactly two client leaves**, both self-contained: `SlideshowControls` (dots + autoplay over a CSS scroll-snap track that still works with JS off) and `InertForm`. `faq` uses native `<details>` and needs no island at all. Newsletter and contact have no backend by design (SPEC §2 rules out marketing email), so they acknowledge the visitor inline rather than posting nowhere.

## Tests (142 in the package, +75)

Written first — I had a 17-failure red bar before implementing. Every section renders with schema defaults **and** with a maximal fixture; null images produce a layout-stable placeholder; stale handles degrade to a skeleton; rich-text sanitizes; faq emits real `<details>`; contact links mailto/tel and honours `showForm`; testimonials expose the rating accessibly.

Three of my initial test expectations were wrong rather than the code (an HTML-escaped apostrophe, and fixtures that deliberately mix a real and a null image) — I corrected the tests.

## Verification went past SSR strings

I wired the theme engine into a real Next dev server temporarily (reverted; `apps/storefront` is untouched in the diff) and confirmed hydration, the newsletter island's inline confirmation, `<details>` toggling, no console errors, and no horizontal overflow at 375px or 1280px. That caught three things static rendering would not have:

- **Autoplay desynced the dots** — it advanced a counter independently of the scroll position. The track is now the single source of truth.
- **Centred `rich-text` lists** stranded their bullet markers far left of the text.
- **A visible native scrollbar** under the slideshow and the product-detail gallery.

## One real bug found in self-review

The slideshow addressed its track by a hard-coded DOM id. A theme with **two slideshows on one page** — which the schema permits and the AI can emit — would produce duplicate ids, and the second slideshow's dots would drive the first one's track. It now uses a React ref, with the slides passed through as `children` so they stay Server Components. I verified the fix with two slideshows on one page: each dot set calls `scrollTo` on its own track and nothing on the other.

I also fixed two defects in F1's own files, both the identical pattern to code added here: the product-detail gallery scrollbar, and the colour-discipline gate not recursing into subdirectories — which meant `sections/client/` would have escaped it entirely.

## Note on what I could not verify

The browser pane runs pages with `visibilityState: "hidden"`, which suppresses rAF-driven smooth scrolling and scroll-event dispatch. I could not watch the carousel animate. I verified the mechanism instead: React hydration (fiber + props present), the click handlers firing with correct targets, and the scroll listener updating `aria-current` via a synthetic scroll event. The animation itself is standard `scrollTo({behavior:'smooth'})`.

Also worth flagging: another agent's dev server was sharing port 3002 and intermittently serving stale pages, which cost me some time before I moved to a private port. Nothing in the repo needs changing for it — just be aware if you see confusing storefront output.

For whoever picks up E2: sections read `data.collectionsByHandle`, `data.productsByHandle` and `data.newestProducts`; anything unresolved degrades to a skeleton, so a stale AI handle is safe. And `globals.css` still needs the `@source` line for the theme-engine — I confirmed locally that without it Tailwind emits none of the section classes, and with it the storefront renders correctly. Both are in `docs/AGENT-LOG.md`.

# Building LLM-Powered Applications with Claude

This skill helps you build LLM-powered applications with Claude. Choose the right surface based on your needs, detect the project language, then read the relevant language-specific documentation.

## Before You Start

Scan the target file (or, if no target file, the prompt and project) for non-Anthropic provider markers - `import openai`, `from openai`, `langchain_openai`, `OpenAI(`, `gpt-4`, `gpt-5`, file names like `agent-openai.py` or `*-generic.py`, or any explicit instruction to keep the code provider-neutral. If you find any, stop and tell the user that this skill produces Claude/Anthropic SDK code; ask whether they want to switch the file to Claude or want a non-Claude implementation. Do not edit a non-Anthropic file with Anthropic SDK calls. (Exception: the `prompt-audit` subcommand is non-interactive and does not stop here - it records non-Anthropic provider markers in its report's stated assumptions and never proposes switching a non-Anthropic file to the Anthropic SDK.)

## Output Requirement

When the user asks you to add, modify, or implement a Claude feature, your code must call Claude through one of:

1. **The official Anthropic SDK** for the project's language (`anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `com.anthropic.*`, etc.). This is the default whenever a supported SDK exists for the project.
2. **Raw HTTP** (`curl`, `requests`, `fetch`, `httpx`, etc.) - only when the user explicitly asks for cURL/REST/raw HTTP, the project is a shell/cURL project, or the language has no official SDK.

Never mix the two - don't reach for `requests`/`fetch` in a Python or TypeScript project just because it feels lighter. Never fall back to OpenAI-compatible shims.

**Never guess SDK usage.** Function names, class names, namespaces, method signatures, and import paths must come from explicit documentation - either the `{lang}/` files in this skill or the official SDK repositories or documentation links listed in `shared/live-sources.md`. If the binding you need is not explicitly documented in the skill files, WebFetch the relevant SDK repo from `shared/live-sources.md` before writing code. Do not infer Ruby/Java/Go/PHP/C# APIs from cURL shapes or from another language's SDK.

**If WebFetch or repository access fails** (network restricted, timeouts, clone blocked): do not keep retrying - write code from the patterns and namespace/package tables in the `{lang}/` file, run the compiler or interpreter on it, and iterate on the error output. For statically-typed SDKs (C#, Java, Go) a compile-fix loop against local errors reaches working code faster than blocked network research.

## Defaults

Unless the user requests otherwise:

For the Claude model version, please use Claude Opus 5, which you can access via the exact model string `claude-opus-5`. Please default to using adaptive thinking (`thinking: {type: "adaptive"}`) for anything remotely complicated. And finally, please default to streaming for any request that may involve long input, long output, or high `max_tokens` - it prevents hitting request timeouts. Use the SDK's `.get_final_message()` / `.finalMessage()` helper to get the complete response if you don't need to handle individual stream events

## Warning: API Drift - Your Training Prior May Be Stale

Several common Claude API shapes changed in 2025-2026. If you recall a pattern from training, verify it against the `{lang}/` files in this skill before writing - the rows below are the most frequent drift points:

| Area | Stale prior | Current API |
|---|---|---|
| Extended thinking | `thinking: {type: "enabled", budget_tokens: N}` | On Claude 4.6+ models: `thinking: {type: "adaptive"}`. `budget_tokens` is deprecated on Opus 4.6 / Sonnet 4.6 and **rejected with a 400** on Fable 5 / Sonnet 5 / Opus 5 / 4.8 / 4.7. Pre-4.6 models still use `budget_tokens`. |
| Web search / web fetch tool type | `web_search_20250305`, `web_fetch_20250910` | `web_search_20260209`, `web_fetch_20260209` (dynamic filtering) on Opus 5/4.8/4.7/4.6, Sonnet 5, and Sonnet 4.6. Older models keep the basic variants; on Vertex AI only basic `web_search_20250305` is available (web fetch is not on Vertex) - see the Server Tools QR below. |
| PHP parameter names | snake_case wire names as named args (`max_tokens`) | Top-level named args are camelCase (`maxTokens`). Nested array keys vary by feature (e.g. `'taskBudget'`, `'skillID'`, `'mcp_server_name'`) - copy the exact key from the documented example; do not bulk-convert. |
| Managed Agents credentials | Keep secrets host-side via custom tools (the only option before vaults shipped) | Vault `environment_variable` credentials - stored by Anthropic, substituted at egress, never visible in the sandbox (`shared/managed-agents-tools.md` -> Vaults). Host-side custom tools remain the fallback for self-hosted sandboxes. |

The `{lang}/` files in this skill are authoritative over recalled patterns.

## Subcommands

If the User Request at the bottom of this prompt is a bare subcommand string (no prose), search every **Subcommands** table in this document - including any in sections appended below - and follow the matching Action column directly. This lets users invoke specific flows via `/claude-api <subcommand>`. If no table in the document matches, treat the request as normal prose.

| Subcommand | Action |
|---|---|
| `migrate` | Migrate existing Claude API code to a newer model. **Read `shared/model-migration.md` immediately** and follow it in order: Step 0 (confirm scope - ask which files/directories before any edit), Step 1 (classify each file), then the per-target breaking-changes section. Do not summarize the guide - execute it. If the user did not name a target model, ask which model to migrate to in the same turn as the scope question. After the per-target changes are applied, audit the in-scope prompt text, tool descriptions, and request code against `shared/prompt-audit.md` - prompting written for the source model is part of every migration, and it does not announce itself. |
| `prompt-audit` | Audit existing prompts, skills, and tool descriptions for dated patterns ("cruft") written for older models. **Read `shared/prompt-audit.md` immediately** and follow it in order: Step 0 (establish scope and target model from the request and the repository - state the assumptions in the report, do not stop to ask), inventory, provenance, then the pattern scan. Produce both deliverables in full - the audit report (findings with `file:line`, pattern, why it's obsolete for the target model, confidence) and a proposed diff - without pausing for confirmation; apply edits only if the request explicitly asked for them. Do not summarize the guide - execute it. |
| `upgrade` | Upgrade the project's Anthropic SDK dependency across a major version - currently the Python SDK, `anthropic` 0.x -> 1.x. Trailing words may name the language and/or a scope (`upgrade python`, `upgrade python sdk src/`). **Read `python/claude-api/sdk-upgrade.md` immediately** and follow it in order: Step 0 (confirm scope, then establish the current and target versions - a published 1.x must exist before you write a pin), the Step 1 inventory, each numbered section, then verification and the report. Do not summarize the guide - execute it. If the detected or named language has no `sdk-upgrade.md` in this skill, say that no major-version upgrade guide is bundled for that SDK yet and point the user at that SDK's CHANGELOG (repositories in `shared/live-sources.md`); do not improvise one from the Python guide. This is not model migration - to move code to a newer Claude model, use `migrate`. |

## Language Detection

Before reading code examples, determine which language the user is working in (exception: for the `prompt-audit` subcommand, skip this section's ask steps - the audit is non-interactive and its inventory is language-agnostic; when no language is inferable, proceed without asking and state the assumption in the report):

1. **Look at project files** to infer the language:

   - `*.py`, `requirements.txt`, `pyproject.toml`, `setup.py`, `Pipfile` -> **Python** - read from `python/`
   - `*.ts`, `*.tsx`, `package.json`, `tsconfig.json` -> **TypeScript** - read from `typescript/`
   - `*.js`, `*.jsx` (no `.ts` files present) -> **TypeScript** - JS uses the same SDK, read from `typescript/`
   - `*.java`, `pom.xml`, `build.gradle` -> **Java** - read from `java/`
   - `*.kt`, `*.kts`, `build.gradle.kts` -> **Java** - Kotlin uses the Java SDK, read from `java/`
   - `*.scala`, `build.sbt` -> **Java** - Scala uses the Java SDK, read from `java/`
   - `*.go`, `go.mod` -> **Go** - read from `go/`
   - `*.rb`, `Gemfile` -> **Ruby** - read from `ruby/`
   - `*.cs`, `*.csproj` -> **C#** - read from `csharp/`
   - `*.php`, `composer.json` -> **PHP** - read from `php/`

2. **If multiple languages detected** (e.g., both Python and TypeScript files):

   - Check which language the user's current file or question relates to
   - If still ambiguous, ask: "I detected both Python and TypeScript files. Which language are you using for the Claude API integration?"

3. **If language can't be inferred** (empty project, no source files, or unsupported language):

   - Use AskUserQuestion with options: Python, TypeScript, Java, Go, Ruby, cURL/raw HTTP, C#, PHP
   - If AskUserQuestion is unavailable, default to Python examples and note: "Showing Python examples. Let me know if you need a different language."

4. **If unsupported language detected** (Rust, Swift, C++, Elixir, etc.):

   - Suggest cURL/raw HTTP examples from `curl/` and note that community SDKs may exist
   - Offer to show Python or TypeScript examples as reference implementations

5. **If user needs cURL/raw HTTP examples**, read from `curl/`.

### Language-Specific Feature Support

Every SDK language above supports both the beta Tool Runner and Managed Agents (beta) - Python (`@beta_tool` decorator), TypeScript (`betaZodTool` + Zod), Java (annotated classes), Go (`BetaToolRunner` in the `toolrunner` pkg), Ruby (`BaseTool` + `tool_runner`), C# (`BetaToolRunner` + raw JSON schema), PHP (`BetaRunnableTool` + `toolRunner()`); code entry points are in the Tool Use Patterns quick reference below. cURL is raw HTTP (no SDK features) and supports Managed Agents.

> **Managed Agents code examples**: see the reading guide in the `## Managed Agents (Beta)` section below.

---

## Which Surface Should I Use?

> **Start simple.** Default to the simplest tier that meets your needs. Single API calls and workflows handle most use cases - only reach for agents when the task genuinely requires open-ended, model-driven exploration. "Simplest" means the least code you own: for a hosted, scheduled, or memory-backed agent, Managed Agents is usually the simplest option (no loop code, no state files, no scheduler), even though it's a bigger platform.

| Use Case                                        | Tier            | Recommended Surface       | Why                                                          |
| ----------------------------------------------- | --------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Classification, summarization, extraction, Q&A  | Single LLM call | **Claude API**            | One request, one response                                    |
| Batch processing or embeddings                  | Single LLM call | **Claude API**            | Specialized endpoints                                        |
| Multi-step pipelines with code-controlled logic | Workflow        | **Claude API + tool use** | You orchestrate the loop                                     |
| Custom agent with your own tools                | Agent           | **Claude API + tool use** | Maximum flexibility                                          |
|

### 🤖 Claude

**F3 — Themes API + AI generation job is merged** ([PR #27](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/27), squashed into `main` as `872fb3b`). CI green, branch deleted.

## What shipped

**`/admin/api/themes`** — version history, `publish`, `restore`, preset apply, signed preview tokens, and the builder conversation, all behind `requirePermission('builder')`.

**The AI loop** — one forced `set_theme` tool call whose input schema *is* `themeDocSchema` (via `zod-to-json-schema`, refs inlined). That means the section catalog and every `.describe()` I wrote into the contract during F1 reach the model as prompt text, and the schema can't drift from what the renderer accepts. Model id comes from config (`claude-sonnet-5`, per SPEC §3 — not the Opus default the API skill recommends).

**Validation is stricter than the schema.** A doc that parses but references a collection the shop doesn't have renders an empty block, which reads as a broken store. So handle-existence is a validation failure too, fed back on the single retry. Then an apologetic chat message — the job never throws, because a thrown job retries on the queue and the merchant watches a spinner forever.

**No key, no problem.** With `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` unset the chat explains itself, returns no job, and presets still apply. Signup also publishes the aurora preset, so a new store opens on a real storefront rather than a blank page.

## Verification

Beyond the 24 new tests, I ran the whole chain against real Postgres and Redis with a deliberately invalid key: signup published aurora → a chat message queued a job → the worker picked it up → the real SDK call returned a genuine 401 → the pending bubble resolved *in place* to the apology. Preset apply → publish → preview token all exercised over HTTP too, and publishing correctly demoted the onboarding theme to leave exactly one published version.

**I could not do the real-key run.** There are no Anthropic credentials on this machine (no `ant` CLI, no env var, empty key in `.env`). So the request was rejected at auth — which means the tool `input_schema` was never validated server-side. It's 46KB, fully inlined with no `$ref`, and the `.describe()` text survives the conversion; that's as far as I could check it. Worth someone running once with a real key.

## The review catch worth naming

`publishVersion` demotes "every other published version" inside a transaction. If the tenant extension didn't reach inside `$transaction`, that `updateMany` would have unpublished **every other shop's live theme** — the unforgivable bug. I wrote a cross-tenant test before assuming either way: the extension does apply, and that invariant is now locked down by a test rather than by hope.

Three smaller fixes from the same pass: appending messages no longer round-trips through the parser (which would have silently deleted any unrecognised history entry), concurrent first-messages converge on one conversation instead of one silently vanishing, and conversations get a `conv_` id prefix instead of borrowing `evt_`.

## One thing to hand off

G1 still hasn't landed, so `services/themes/generation.ts` carries a **temporary private BullMQ producer** for the `ai` queue. It uses WS-G's exact queue and job names, so when [#11](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/11) merges the body collapses to a single `enqueue(...)` call. That's flagged in `docs/AGENT-LOG.md` for WS-G, along with the two-line signup touch for WS-A and the F4 API surface.

For F4 (the builder UI) you now need only A3 — the admin shell — which is still unclaimed and blocking eight issues.

        <span
          key={color}""","""      {colors.map((color, index) => (
        <span
          // biome-ignore lint/suspicious/noArrayIndexKey: a palette repeats colours — monochrome's primary and text are both #111111 — so the value is not a key
          key={index}""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
print('patched')
PY
export PATH="$HOME/.nvm/versions/node/v

### 👤 User

`minimal prompt → single careful diff pass → ≤15 findings`

You are reviewing a pull request for real bugs. Run `git diff @{upstream}...HEAD` (or `git diff main...HEAD` / `git diff HEAD~1`
if there's no upstream) to get the unified diff under review. If there are
uncommitted changes, or the range diff is empty, also run `git diff HEAD` and
include the working-tree changes in scope — the review often runs before the
commit. If a PR number, branch name, or file path was passed as an argument,
review that target instead. Treat this diff as the review scope.

Review the diff as a careful senior engineer would: read every hunk, open the surrounding files for context as needed (Read, Grep, git log/blame/show), and hunt for correctness issues — wrong or inverted conditions, off-by-one, null/undefined dereference, missing `await`, dropped error handling, removed guards or validations, broken callers of changed functions, races. Prefer real failure modes over style; every finding needs a concrete scenario in which the code misbehaves.

When you are done, submit at most 15 findings via the ReportFindings tool, filling its fields as defined — for each: the file path and start line, a severity, and a comment that states the issue and the concrete scenario in which the code misbehaves. Quality over quantity: include everything you genuinely believe is a real issue, and nothing you don't.

After the tool call, also restate the findings in your final reply — one line each, `file:line — summary` — so they stay visible in sessions that do not render tool output.

### 🤖 Claude

**F4 is merged** ([PR #44](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/44), squashed into `main` as `fbc10a7`) — and with it **workstream F is complete**: F1 theme engine, F2 marketing sections, F3 themes API + AI job, F4 builder UI.

## What shipped

The split-screen builder behind the Storefront nav item:

- **Chat panel** over F3's conversation — history, animated pending state while a job runs, "View this version" chip on assistant messages that produced one. Polling is 2s, only while something is pending, and gives up after three minutes so a dead worker can't leave an open tab hammering the API.
- **Preview pane** — an iframe of the *real* storefront origin with F3's signed `?preview=` token, so the preview can't drift from what ships. Device toggle, page switcher deep-linked to a real seeded handle, refresh, and a draft-vs-live indicator.
- **Publish** — confirm → "Theme published" toast → token dropped so the iframe shows what shoppers get.
- **Version history** with badges, snippets, Preview and Restore.
- **Preset picker** with swatches read from the presets themselves, always visible — so the no-key path needs no detection and never dead-ends.

## Verification

Walked H2 flow (d) by hand against the seeded stack: applied bloom (2→3 versions, preview auto-followed the new draft with a fresh 15-minute token scoped to it), published it (Live badge, toast, token dropped, **exactly one published version across all 17 shops** in the database), restored an older version, exercised the device toggle and page switcher. Console clean.

Review caught four real issues, all fixed: a failed send silently swallowed the error *and* discarded the merchant's typed message; switching versions reloaded the iframe twice; a draft's iframe rendered before its token arrived, flashing the published theme; and duplicate swatch keys (monochrome's primary and text are both `#111111`).

## Three things worth flagging

**The preview currently shows E2's placeholder page.** The storefront has no `/`, `/products/[handle]` or `/collections/[handle]` routes yet — E2 hasn't landed. My side is verified end to end (correct origin, tenant resolved from Host, signed token accepted), so real themed pages appear the moment E2 merges, with no change here. Noted in `docs/AGENT-LOG.md` for WS-E.

**I couldn't use the intended browser setup.** The in-app browser pane blocks every subresource on `*.lvh.me` (`ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT`), and the Chrome extension isn't connected. I verified against a localhost-origin stack instead (`ADMIN_URL`/`API_URL`/`STOREFRONT_BASE_DOMAIN` overridden at runtime only — no code changes), which exercised the same code paths including cross-origin cookies and tenant resolution via `demo.localhost:3002`.

**Two cleanups along the way.** A stale `scripts/stack.sh` change was sitting staged in my worktree from an earlier session; main already had the real fix and [#34](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/34) landed the same hardening, so I dropped it rather than smuggle it into this PR. And my branch had replayed F3's commits as near-no-ops after the squash-merge — I reset onto main so the PR contains only F4.

I also left the demo store on the seeded aurora theme rather than the bloom I published while testing. `pnpm db:reset` asks for explicit consent before dropping data, which I didn't have, so I republished the original through the API instead — the two test drafts remain in version history, which is just what a merchant's history looks like.

The board is now clear of open PRs. The unclaimed issues that unblock the most are **A4** (settings hub) and the remaining UI work — **B5** products, **C5** orders, **C6** customers/discounts, **D4** payments — all of which A3 unblocked. **H2** (the Playwright smoke suite) still needs B5, C5 and E4 before it can run its five flows.

>   - **The in-app browser only renders `localhost` origins.** It loads the HTML of an `*.lvh.me` page but blocks every subresource (`ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT`), so the admin and storefront come up unstyled and never hydrate — which looks like a broken page, not a blocked one. Verified on `admin.lvh.me:3000` and `d

## F4 — yes, merged

[PR #44](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/44), squashed as `fbc10a7`. That completes workstream F: theme engine, marketing sections, themes API + AI job, builder UI.

## The browser problem — root-caused and fixed

**It was fixable, and it wasn't really about F4.**

The in-app browser only renders `localhost` origins. An `*.lvh.me` page returns its HTML fine, but every subresource is blocked client-side (`ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT`), so the admin came up unstyled and never hydrated — which looks like a broken app rather than a blocked one. That's what sent me down the wrong path initially.

I checked the two plausible causes before concluding:

- **Was it WS-A's `allowedDevOrigins` fix?** No — that landed after my first attempt, and retesting with it in place changed nothing. It's a client-side block, not a server response.
- **Was it target-based or origin-based?** Origin-based. From an `admin.lvh.me` page, *every* fetch was blocked — including to `localhost:3000`. From a `localhost` page, everything worked.

The confirming detail: `preview_start({name: "dev"})` reports `configUrl.outcome: "failed"` on `admin.lvh.me` and silently falls back to localhost. Three of the four `launch.json` targets pointed at `*.lvh.me`, so CLAUDE.md's "see the app" flow — the thing it says pixel-parity work *should* use — didn't work for any agent on the swarm.

I also made a genuine process mistake worth owning: I started the stack with Bash instead of `preview_start`, which the tool docs explicitly warn against. That wasn't the cause here, but it delayed finding it.

**The fix** ([PR #48](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/48), `02303a6`) is 16 lines and strictly additive, per your "no breaking changes":

- `pnpm dev:localhost` — the same `turbo run dev` with three env overrides (all three already in turbo.json's `globalEnv`).
- `dev-localhost` and `storefront-localhost` launch targets.
- The constraint and recipe documented in CLAUDE.md §1.

§6 still holds under the overrides: admin and API stay same-site so the session cookie survives, and `{slug}.localhost` resolves a tenant exactly like `{slug}.lvh.me`. **Nothing existing changed** — `.env`, `.env.example`, the original four targets and `pnpm dev` are all untouched.

Verified rather than assumed: `dev-localhost` opens (`outcome: "seeded"` vs `"failed"`), login succeeds and `/auth/me` returns 200 with the demo shop — proving the cookie survives — and `storefront-localhost` gets 2 stylesheets on `demo.localhost:3002` where `demo.lvh.me:3002` gets 0.

One caveat on the workaround: it's for *looking* at the app. Anything that depends on the real hostnames — a wildcard-subdomain bug, cookie-domain behaviour on `lvh.me` — still needs `pnpm dev` and a real browser. I noted that distinction in CLAUDE.md rather than implying localhost is equivalent.

Main has moved a lot meanwhile — B5 (products admin UI), C3, E3, G2 all landed. **A4 (settings hub)** is the notable unclaimed issue now.

### 🤖 Claude

**Both are fully unblocked** — I verified against the code, not just the index:

- **C5** needs A3, C2, C3. All the endpoints exist: `GET /`, `GET /:id`, `PATCH /:id`, `POST /:id/cancel`, `/events`, `/fulfillments`, `/refunds`, `/refunds/calculate`.
- **E4** needs E3, D1. Checkout lifecycle is there including `POST /checkouts/:token/complete` (plus `completeCheckout` in the service layer), and `POST /vault/tokenize` landed with D1.

## Pick C5

The deciding factor is that **E2 is in flight right now, and E4 lives in the same app**:

- E2 owns `apps/storefront/**` *except* checkout routes
- E4 owns `apps/storefront/src/app/checkouts/**`
- C5 owns `apps/admin/src/app/store/[slug]/orders/**`

On paper E2 and E4 are separable, but in practice they'll collide: E4's checkout pages need the storefront layout, `globals.css`, the theme token wiring and probably shared cart/line-item components — all of which E2 is actively creating and reshaping right now. Starting E4 today means either rebasing against a moving foundation or stubbing the very things E2 is defining. C5 is in a different app entirely, so it can't race.

Two smaller points in the same direction:

**C5 is the stronger KPI work.** Order detail is one of the most recognizable Shopify screens, and PARITY.md specifies it precisely — header `#1001` + date + Paid/Unfulfilled badges, fulfillment card, payment card with Subtotal/Shipping/Tax then bold Total, Timeline with a comment box, right column Notes/Customer, `Refund` + `More actions ▾`. That detail removes most of the design ambiguity.

**The admin patterns are fresh.** B5 (products index + form) just landed, so the IndexTable + tabs + filters + contextual save bar conventions are already proven in-repo to copy from.

One thing worth knowing either way: **H2 needs both C5 and E4**, so whichever you don't take still has to happen before the smoke suite can run. C5 now, E4 once E2 has settled, is the sequencing that avoids the collision.

If you'd rather I take one of these while you do the other, say which and I'll claim it.

### 🤖 Claude

**C5 is merged** — [PR #56](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/56), squashed as `2b9c3f0`.

## What shipped

Four screens, built to PARITY.md rather than to memory:

- **Index** — IndexTable with Shopify's tabs (All/Unfulfilled/Unpaid/Open/Closed), search, sort, cursor pagination at 50, and the payment + fulfillment badge pair per row.
- **Detail** — order number, placed date and status badges in the header; left column of fulfillment cards (unfulfilled items carrying "Fulfill items", then a card per shipment with tracking), payment card, and a Timeline with a comment composer; right column of Notes, Customer, contact and both addresses. Cancel confirms with a reason and disables once money is captured, as Shopify's does.
- **Fulfil** — quantity per line defaulting to what's left, location select, optional tracking, and a button that counts what it will ship.
- **Refund** — line pickers, restock, shipping field, and a live summary that comes entirely from `refunds/calculate`. The button carries the amount.
- **Orders nav badge**, wired through the `openOrders` prop A3 had already designed for it.

Badge wording lives in `_components/status.ts` as data, pinned by a test — the enums are snake_case internals and `partially_refunded` must never reach a merchant's screen.

## The manual walk was worth doing

I ran the whole of H2 flow (b) on seeded data: fulfil 1 of 3 items → "Partially fulfilled" → refund that item → "Partially refunded", timeline showing both events. It caught **three bugs that all typechecked fine**:

1. **"Paid by customer $0.00" on a refunded order.** A refund flips the payment's status away from `captured`, and I was only counting `captured` — so it also showed the full $427.49 as outstanding. Fixed with a tested `capturedTotal` helper.
2. **The refund idempotency key was regenerated on failure.** A retry after a timeout would have refunded the customer twice — the exact thing the key exists to prevent. It's now derived from the refund itself.
3. **The sort control sent `sortDirection` where the contract says `sortOrder`**, so direction was silently ignored.

I also debounced the calculate call (it was firing per keystroke in the shipping field).

## Two things worth knowing

**A 409 I hit was not mine.** Refunding any seeded order failed with `conflict: Unknown transaction mock_ch_1040` — the mock processor's in-memory ledger doesn't know about payments the seed wrote directly. That was [PR #51](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/51), already merged; I rebased onto it and the refund went through. Flagged for H2 in the log, since the smoke suite will hit the same thing on an older main.

**The browser pane kept dropping session cookies** across page loads, and simulated typing didn't reach the page at all because the pane wasn't compositing. I worked around it by authenticating via the page's own fetch and driving the React handlers directly — which is why some verification is DOM-level rather than screenshots. The index page screenshot did come out, and it looks right.

Also worth noting: my worktree database was two migrations behind, which surfaced as unrelated `db` and `api` test failures until I ran `migrate:deploy`. If another agent sees Prisma validation errors out of nowhere, that's the cause.

The board is nearly clear — **29 issues done**. What's left: **A5** (production stack, Day 2), **C6** (customers + discounts UI), **D4** (payments settings UI), **E4** (checkout UI), **E5** (customer accounts), **G4** (apps + admin REST API), then **H2** (Playwright smoke — now only waiting on E4) and **H3**.

### 🤖 Claude

Both answered.

## C6 → yes, that completes workstream C

C has exactly six issues. C1–C5 are merged; C6 is the last one. When it lands, **C is done**.

## The cookie drops: concurrent agents, and I reproduced it

It was **not** the browser. Here's the mechanism, confirmed by experiment rather than inference:

Every worktree shares **one `SESSION_SECRET`** (identical `4cdb4192da21` across all 13), but `pnpm worktree:env` gives each its **own Redis slot** — mine is `/8`, others hold `/0`–`/12`. Sessions live in Redis.

So when another worktree's stack takes port 3001:
1. Your cookie still passes signature verification against *their* API — same secret.
2. The session id then misses in *their* Redis.
3. You get `401 unauthorized: "Your session has expired. Sign in again."` and the shell bounces you to `/login`.

Nothing is wrong with your session or your code — you're talking to someone else's stack.

**The proof:** I ran two API processes identical in every way except `REDIS_URL`, and sent the same cookie to both:

| API | Redis slot | `/auth/me` |
|---|---|---|
| A | `/8` (mine) | **200** |
| B | `/9` (simulated other worktree) | **401 "Your session has expired"** |

And the direct evidence it was happening live: while I was testing, `lsof` showed port 3000 held by `resolve-docs-issue-3f28ad/apps/admin` — a different worktree entirely. `pnpm stack status` says it outright: *"served by worktree 'resolve-docs-issue-3f28ad', not main."*

That also explains every symptom I'd been treating as flakiness: login succeeded then died seconds later, curl held a session fine during quiet windows, and the session never actually expired server-side (TTL is 7 days).

## Is it fixable?

**Properly, yes** — and most of the machinery already exists. `API_PORT`/`ADMIN_PORT`/`STOREFRONT_PORT` are already in the env schema, the API already honours `API_PORT`, and `worktree-env.sh` already computes a per-worktree slot it could derive ports from. Two things block it: admin and storefront **hardcode** `next dev --port 3000` / `--port 3002` in their package.json, and every doc, `.claude/launch.json` target and the not-yet-written H2 Playwright config assumes the fixed ports.

**I didn't do it**, deliberately. Re-porting the swarm would change the documented URLs for twelve agents mid-flight and pre-empt a decision H2 will depend on — exactly the kind of cross-cutting change that breaks other people's work. It's a coordinated root change, not a drive-by.

What I did instead ([PR #58](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/58), merged) was close the diagnosis gap: CLAUDE.md now connects the symptom to the cause, right beside the existing "ports are shared" note. The failure was expensive precisely because it's invisible — it looks like an auth bug, and `pnpm stack status` already names the culprit if you know to run it.

If you want the real fix, the safe shape is: make the two `--port` flags read `${ADMIN_PORT:-3000}` (a no-op at today's defaults), then add an opt-in `--ports` flag to `worktree-env.sh` that assigns slot-derived ports. That leaves everyone unaffected unless they opt in. Say the word and I'll do it.

### 🤖 Claude

I checked, and the answer is: **it would do nothing we need, and I was wrong to propose it.** Withdrawing it.

## What it would have done

Given each worktree its own ports, so all twelve agents could run stacks simultaneously without stealing `:3001` from each other — no port contention, no phantom logouts.

## Why we don't need it

**It's already a logged decision, with a rationale I failed to check.** `DECISIONS.md` line 78:

> Ports 3000/3001/3002 stay **SHARED on purpose**: the collision is the only thing stopping nine Next dev stacks from running at once on an 8 GB machine that already pages.

The port collision isn't a bug in the design — it *is* the design. It's functioning as a mutex that limits the machine to one dev stack at a time.

And the rationale isn't hypothetical. I measured this machine:

```
8.0 GB physical
swap: 11.2 GB total, 9.9 GB used   ← already paging hard
49 node processes running
```

The machine is deep into swap *with the single-stack arrangement working as intended*. Twelve worktrees × (admin + storefront + api + worker) is roughly 48 dev servers, each Next instance being the expensive part. My fix would have removed the only thing preventing that.

CLAUDE.md §3 is explicit — "Never relitigate a logged decision" — and that's exactly what I was doing. I proposed it before reading `DECISIONS.md` on this point, which is my mistake.

## What the real cost of the status quo is

Now that the symptom is documented, an agent who gets logged out mid-test runs `pnpm stack status`, sees *"served by worktree 'X', not main"*, runs `pnpm stack up`, and continues. Call it thirty seconds.

The expensive part was never the contention — it was not knowing that a 401 saying "Your session has expired" meant "another agent owns your port." That's what cost me time, and that's what [PR #58](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/58) fixed.

So the documentation wasn't the consolation prize for not doing the real fix. It *was* the fix.

The only thing that would change this calculus is different hardware — a bigger machine, or agents on separate hosts. That'd be a fresh decision for whoever owns the infra, logged in `DECISIONS.md` rather than worked around in a package.json.
